Counsel for the Appellant: GP FOR APPEALS
Counsel for the Respondents: SRI E' VIKRAM REDDY
The Couerr delivered the following: COMMON JUDGMENT

HON'BLE SRI JUSTICE P.NAVEEN RAO
AND
HON'BLE SRI JUSTICE SAMBASIVARAO NAIDU
LAND ACOI'ISITION APPEAL SUIT Nos.129. 140. 145. 148. 151 & 152
COMMON JUDGMENT : fper Hon'ble Si Justice P.Naueen Rao\
These appeals are filed by the Land Acquisition Oflicer (LAO),
representing the state challenging the orders passed by the reference court
in the respective O.Ps. As the issires involved in these appeals are same, all
the appeals are heard together and disposed ofby this common order.
2. Heard -iearned Government $eader for Appeals appearing for the
appellants in all these appeals and sri K.Rajanna, learned counsel
appearing for the respondents in all these appeals.
3. The notilication under section a (i) of the Land Acquisition Act, 1984
(for short 'the Act') was issued on 18.01.2008, declaration was published on
21.01.2008, possession of the land was taken on 24.01.2010 and Award
was passed on 25.10.2010. In all 68 structures are involved and the LAo
determined the total amount of compensation payabre in all these 6g
structures as Rs.2,66,48,47T l-. Not satisfied with the compensation
determined by the LAo on the structures, owners of the properties sought
reference under Section 18 of the Act. The references are numbered as
O.P.Nos.64, 72, 65, 66, TZ & T4 of 2OI2. Though separate orders are
passed by the reference court in individual o.ps, the direction portion in all
the O.Ps, is same. The State is in appeal challenging the Awards with
-2-
(
oF 2016

-3 -
reference to the extent of Paragraph Nos.l and 2 under point No.2.
read as under :
"1. The compensation amount awarded by Land Acquisition
Officer on the structures / residential houses is hereby
enhanced as doubled. 2. The claimants shall be entitled to additional amount of
compensation @12o/o on the enhanced market value from the
date of Sec.4(1) notihcation till the date of taking over
possession or till the date of award rvhichever is ear'lier."
4. According to learned Government Pleader, the reference Court erred in
enhancing the structural value by 100% without considering the assessment
made by the LAO. At any cost there cannot be 100% enhanccment of the
compensation payable on structures. Learned Government Pleader also
contends that the reference Court grossly erred in awarding 12% additional
amount on the enhanced market value, whereas 12% additional amount is
payable on the original amount of compensation determined by the LAO.
Therefore, point No.2 is contrary to the provision in Section 23 (1-A) of the
Act.
5. By relying on the additional ground raised, learned Government
Pleader contends that in addition to the compensation awarded by the LAO,
the claimants have also received certain perks and the same are not
mentioned and it would clearly amount to suppression of material fact
which should have weighed with the reference Court while considering the
O.Ps.
6. We have gone through the record. As fairly submitted by learned
Government Pleader, valuation of structures was made by the Agriculture
Department, whereas, ordinarily, the Roads & Buildings Department makes
They

-4-
structural assessment based on standard specifications. Be that as it may,
C
the strucrural assessment made by Agriculture Department is not faulted by
the claimants on this ground. The LAO placed reiiance on the said
assessment to determine compensation payable to the claimants, whereas,
the reference court placed reliance on the assessment made by a private
valuer while determining the corhpensation and enhancing the
compensation by 100%.
7. From the evidence on record, as noticed by the reference court, it is
seen that as deposed by Pw.S, he suspects foul pray with regard to the
estimates prepared by the LAo, got estimates made by private valuer and
such estimates were submitted to the Special Deputy collector, when he
visited Gram Panchayat of |,iamnoor Village. This factum is not disputed by
learned Government Pleader. The record would disclose that the Special
Deputy collector was aware of the estimations obtained by the ciaimants
from private engineer. There is no discussion in the Award on the validity of
the said estimations made. It is also relevant to note that though the LAo
placed reliance on the assessment made by the Agriculture Department to
estimate the value of properties, those assessments were not marked before
the reference court. on the contrary the estimations made by the claimants
through private engineer were marked as Ex.A. 13 (1) to (6g) and the same
was taken into consideration.
8. In the absence of any other material, the reference court rightly relied
upon the evidence brought on record on behalf of the claimants on the
actual estimation of the properties belonged to them which rvas sought to be

J
acquired by the state. Therefore, we do not see any error in the assessment
made by the reference court in enhancing compensation by 100%. No other
material is placed before this court to show that the enhancement granted
by the reference court was erroneous and that assessment made by the
private engineer was not correct. In the absence of any other material, the
view taken by the reference court cannot be faulted, for this court to set
aside and declare such assessment as not valid in law. Therefore, this
contention of learned Government Pleader is rejected.
9 . We also see no merit in the contention of learned Government pleader
on the second point in the order of reference Court
10. Section 23 (1-A) reads as under
"ln addition to the market value of the land, as above provided, the
court shall in every case award an amount calculated at the rate of
tweive per centum per annum on such market value for the period
commencing on and from the date of the publication of the
notification under Section 4, sub-section (1), in respect of such land, to the date of the award of the Collector or the date of taking
possession of the iand, whichever is earlie/'
Explanation : In computing the period referred to in this sub-section
any period or periods during which the proceedings for the
acquisition of the land were held up on account ol any stay or
injunction by the order of any court shall be excluded."
I 1. According to this provision, the Court can award amount calculated at
l2o/o p.2., on the market value already determined for the period
commencing from the date of publication of notification under Section 4 (1)
to the date of Award of the Collector, or the date ol taking possession,
whichever is earlier.

( -6-
12. As the claimants are irot satisfied with the compensation determined
L o, ,n" LAo, they sought for reference' on evaluation of the evidence
' brought on record, the reference Court found that the compensation
determined by the LAO was far less than the actual amount payable on the
properties belonging to the ciaimants and therefore' enhanced the
compensation by i00%' The enhanced compensation becomes the actual
marketvalueoftheiands/propertiesacquiredbytheStateandthismarket
vaiue is relatable to the date of Section 4 (1) notification' There is always a
gap between Section 4 (1) notification and taking possession or passing ol
Award. For the delay caused in taking possession or passing ol Au'ard the
owner of land cannot suffer. Therefore, Section 23 (1-A) is introduced to
safeguard the interest of the owners of the properties that were compulsorily
acquired by the State. Thus, Section 23 (1-A) would make it clear that on
the market value determined by the reference court, the claimants are
entitled to interest at the rate of l2o/o p.a., on the said market value- The
second part of the answer by the reference Court is in tune with the
mandate of section 23 (1-A). Therefore, we do not see any error in the said
Iindings of the reference Court.
13. Finally, on the contention of learned Government Pleader that certatn
perks given to the owners of properties, were not taken into consideration, it
is seen that this was not even pleaded before the reference court or before
this Court and also merits no consideration. It is not brought to our notice
that ali this was part of settlement arrived between the parties to resolve
inter se dispute amicably. That being so, merely because perks are paid by
b

-7
the state and received by the claimants, do not take away their right to seek
enhancement of compensation under Section 1g of the Act. Once a
reference is sought under section 18 of the Act, the reference court can
enhance the compensation and t-here on section 23 (1-A) comes into
operation as a matter of course.
14. We therefore, see no merit in the appeals. The Appeals are
accordingly, dismissed. Pending miscellaneous petitions, if any, shali stand
closed.
SD/-K.SRINIVASA RAO
